## Done when

- **`findra` on a machine with no `config.json` shows the first-run screen, and it is the front door.** Three presets whose sizes add up, the free documents row printed as free of charge and not free of consent, Hebrew nested under Speech and absent where the system has no Hebrew, the update-check disclosure beside the downloads, and a "Not now" that leaves a working product and does not come back.
- **Choosing "Just names" after trying another preset leaves content indexing off.** `JustNamesTurnsContentIndexingBackOffAfterAnotherPresetTurnedItOn` is the test, and the failure it prevents is every drive walked and every document opened for somebody who declined.
- **The one elevated thing gets registered, and it answers in the same session.** Whatever was chosen, including nothing, `HelperTask.Register` runs - which nothing in this project called before - and `EnsureRunning` follows it, so name search works without signing out. A refusal is visible, logged, and recoverable from Settings.
- **A dropped network during a download says so and keeps what it fetched.** `ADownloadThatThrowsIsReportedRatherThanThrown` and `OneFileThatFailsDoesNotStopTheNextOne` say so, and `FirstRunState.Problem` is what the screen shows.
- **`findra --models install recommended` and `findra --content on` still work exactly as Plan 5 shipped them.** The screen is a second home, never a replacement.
- **Settings opens from the tray and from the capsule's right-click, on a section rail, in the card's own hand.** Five sections, a fixed-height pane that fits its notes as well as its rows, and the exclusions list as the only scroller anywhere in either surface.
- **Every control either surface draws does something when it is clicked, and three tests say so at three levels.** `EveryDrawnControlDoesSomethingWhenItIsClicked` walks every row of every section; `EveryThingYouCanTouchOnThisScreenDoesSomething` does the same for the first-run screen; `EveryActionReachesTheHostExactlyOnce` closes the loop from the answer to the machine. **No control anywhere is dispatched by its display string**, and `NoControlIsDispatchedByItsLabel` is what keeps it that way.
- **Every setting written by those windows is one the rest of the product actually reads.** A palette change moves the window, the capsule and the tray icon without a restart; a drive choice reaches `Config.IndexDrives` with empty meaning every fixed volume; a folder added through the OS dialog is one `FileKinds.Excluded` says yes to and its sibling no; a chord that will not register is not saved and says which combination was taken; the autostart toggle writes and removes a `Run` value; a capability button starts the same download the first screen starts.
- **Turning the update check off means no request is made, from either surface, including a forced one.** Two tests, one per surface, both driving the real `UpdateCheck.CheckAsync` with a fetch that fails the test if it is ever called.
- **Every surface draws in Quicksand, and the licence ships with it.** `Parts.Face` is the only place in `src/Findra` that resolves a typeface, the card and the capsule and the shot command all take it, a missing resource falls back to the system default rather than stopping the application, and `assets/fonts/OFL.txt` reaches the publish folder and therefore the installer. Until this lands, every README screenshot is drawn in whatever face the machine resolved.
- **`--searchshot` renders sixteen states in six palettes and all ninety-six have colour in them.** Seven of the sixteen are new, and every section of the settings window has one.
- **`findra --uninstall` removes the `HighestAvailable` scheduled task, the autostart entry and the running processes - the processes first - and keeps the models, the index, the logs and the settings.** It states the **measured** size it would free, it tries to remove the task even when it could not tell whether the task is there, it never stops itself, and **it never stops the parent that elevated it**.
- **`--purge` deletes exactly `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Findra` and `%APPDATA%\Findra`, refuses anything outside them before the deleter is ever called, and carries on past one folder that will not go.** `APathOutsideFindrasOwnFoldersIsRefusedAndNeverHandedToTheDeleter`, `ARootIsNeverItsOwnChild` and `OneFolderThatWillNotGoDoesNotStopTheRest` are the three.
- **An unelevated `--uninstall` asks for elevation once and then stops rather than looping**, and `--dry-run` needs no elevation at all.
- **The installer requires administrator rights, puts no version in the install path, stops all three processes before it replaces a file, carries no model, writes no autostart entry of its own, records how Findra arrived, and offers a real unticked checkbox above a report carrying the measured sizes.** Twelve text assertions, because `iscc` is not on this machine and the first compile is in CI.
- **The version lives in `Directory.Build.props`, no project file overrides it, and the number the running build reports parses** - `UpdateCheck.Compare(BuildInfo.Version, "999.0.0") < 0` is the assertion, and it is the one that stops the update check telling everybody they are current for ever.
- **A tag that is not `v<major>.<minor>.<patch>`, or carries a pre-release suffix, or disagrees with the props file, or has no non-empty `CHANGELOG.md` section, fails the release** - each with its own exit code and its own test, run through the same script the workflow runs.
- **Every push builds with `-warnaserror`, tests, publishes self-contained and answers every headless mode**, no workflow spells an environment variable in a way PowerShell mis-parses, and every embedded PowerShell block parses.
- **A tag builds `win-x64` and `win-arm64`, attaches both installers to one release, and takes its notes from the changelog rather than from the commits.** The signing step exists, does nothing, and says so; nothing anywhere claims the binaries are signed, **including the policy page**, which carries a status note coupled to that step by a test.
- **Nothing but a person on the Actions tab can publish to the winget catalogue**, the manifests that get published are the ones this repository keeps, and no placeholder hash can reach it.
- **The README carries at least four real renders, each with the command that produced it, a benchmark fragment with its machine block, the measured 2.93 GB rather than the floor total, the attribution above the fold, and no throughput rate from a default-sized run.**
- **`PRIVACY.md` no longer says that deleting two folders by hand removes everything**, and both policy pages have tests coupling their promises to the code.
- `dotnet test` green, `dotnet build -warnaserror` clean, `grep -ric prism src/ tests/ installer/ packaging/ .github/ docs/` = 0, no em-dashes, no emoji, and a human-read comment pass over the fourteen new source files done and reported.

---

## Why each test can fail

A test whose assertion is satisfied by the fallback path is a defect in the plan, not in the implementation: it reports green while the behaviour it names is broken, and it is worse than no test because it stops anyone writing the real one. **A test that cannot pass at all is worse still** - whoever executes the plan will make it green, and the cheapest way to do that is to delete the assertion that had the teeth.

The first draft of this plan shipped five of those, and the review found them by working the arithmetic and the regular expressions by hand against code in the same document. All five are fixed here and each is named in its own row below. **If you change or add a test while executing this plan, add its row.** If you cannot name an input that makes it fail, say so rather than leaving it.

Four shapes were watched for:

- **Tests that assert a fallback.** "It kept the old hotkey", "it fell back to the source install route", "it did not delete the models" are all satisfied by an implementation that does nothing. Every one below is paired with its opposite - the chord that DOES register is saved, the marker that IS there is read, `--purge` DOES delete, the deleter IS called for a path inside the roots - and the pair is what has teeth.
- **Tests that assert a shape rather than a behaviour.** The rejected draft's structural test proved the painter and the hit test agreed about how many rows there were, and five drawn controls did nothing. Three sweeps now assert behaviour at three levels: the row answers, the screen answers, the action arrives.
- **Tests that read a file for a string.** Twenty-odd of them read a workflow, a manifest, an installer script, a policy page or the README. Each names the edit that breaks it, and where the string might never appear anyway it is marked **guard**.
- **Tests whose arithmetic was never run.** Every geometric assertion in this plan has been worked through by hand against the constants the same task declares, and the numbers are written down beside them so the next reader can check rather than trust.

**There is no concurrency test in this plan**, deliberately. Nothing here is a race: both surfaces run on the UI thread, `FirstRunDownloads` is one flow, and the one genuine cross-thread hazard - the capability gate against the writer connection - is designed out by giving the controller no `ContentDb` at all rather than tested for.

### Task 1 - `VersionTests` (6 methods, 9 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheVersionIsDeclaredInDirectoryBuildPropsAndNowhereElse` | **the state before this task** - `Findra.csproj` holds all three version properties. Also fires if one is added back, which MSBuild would silently prefer over the props file |
| `TheRunningBuildReportsTheVersionThePropsFileDeclares` | a project file overrides the props value, or `Log.Version` keeps reading `AssemblyName.Version` |
| `TheVersionThisBuildReportsIsOneTheUpdateCheckCanActuallyCompare` | **`IncludeSourceRevisionInInformationalVersion` is left at its default** and the `+sha` is not stripped - `Compare` then returns 0 for both assertions, which is the state that tells every user they are current for ever |
| `BuildMetadataIsStrippedSoTheNumberStaysComparable` (4) | `Normalise` returns its input untouched, truncates to two components, or throws on a null informational version |
| `APreReleaseSuffixIsLeftInPlaceRatherThanQuietlyTurnedIntoTheRelease` | the implementation strips at the first `-` as well as the first `+`, and `1.2.0-rc.1` reports itself as the release |
| `AVersionThatCouldNotBeReadIsNotReportedAsZero` | `Normalise` answers `"0.0.0"`, which compares older than every release and puts a permanent update banner in the tray |

### Task 1 - `ReleaseCheckTests` (8 methods, 10 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `AMatchingTagPrintsItsOwnSectionAndStopsAtTheNext` | **the script prints from the heading to the end of the file** - the obvious first implementation - putting the previous release's notes into this release's body |
| `TheLinkDefinitionsAtTheBottomAreNotPartOfTheOldestSectionsNotes` | reference definitions are not skipped; releasing the oldest section publishes a body ending in bare URLs |
| `ATagThatDisagreesWithTheDeclaredVersionIsRefusedAndBothNumbersAreNamed` | the version check is missing, or names one number and the person cannot tell which to change |
| `APreReleaseTagIsRefusedAndSaysWhichCodeCannotHandleIt` | **the regex is `^v\d+\.\d+\.\d+` with no end anchor**, so `v1.2.0-rc.1` passes and a release is published that no installed copy will ever see |
| `ATagThatIsNotAVersionTagIsRefused` (3) | the leading `v` is optional, a two-part version is accepted, or anything containing a version is accepted |
| `ATagWithNoSectionOfItsOwnIsRefused` | the changelog check is missing entirely and the release ships with an empty body |
| `AnEmptySectionIsNotReleaseNotes` | **the check is a single grep for the heading** - a heading with nothing under it publishes an empty release |
| `TheUnreleasedSectionIsNeverUsedAsTheNotesForATag` | the heading matcher accepts any `## [`, and every tag ships with whatever is at the top of the file |

### Task 2 - `RailTests` (20 methods, 22 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheRailAndThePaneDoNotOverlap` | `RailW` is widened to fit a longer title; the pane's first control then sits under the rail, which answers first, and can never be clicked |
| `EverySectionRowSitsInsideTheRailAndBelowTheOneBeforeIt` | `RowH` is smaller than the row it lays out, so two sections share pixels |
| `AClickOnASectionSelectsThatSectionAndNotItsNeighbour` | the rail's top offset is dropped and every click lands one row low |
| `AClickBelowTheLastSectionSelectsNothing` | **the hit test is `(int)((y - top) / RowH)` unbounded** - it answers 5, 6 or 7 for the empty rail, and the caller indexes `Sections` with it |
| `AClickInThePaneOnAPlainRowAnswersAsThatRowAndNotAsAnOption` | a row with no options reports option 0, and `Apply` toggles the first thing in a list the row has not got |
| `AClickOnAnOptionAnswersWithItsOwnIndex` | the option loop is skipped and every swatch click reads as a row click |
| `OptionsInOneRowNeitherOverlapNorEscapeTheRow` (3) | **the width is divided by the count without subtracting the gaps** - the last option runs past the right edge and its centre misses |
| `ARowWithANoteAboveItIsPushedDownByExactlyWhatThatNoteNeeds` | rows are spaced by a fixed gap. The About disclosure is four lines and 66px; at a 14px gap it is drawn over the two rows beneath it, and a row-count check sees nothing wrong |
| `ANoteIsDrawnEntirelyBetweenItsOwnRowAndTheNextOne` | `NoteRect` overlaps either neighbour, or reserves less than `NoteHeight` asks for |
| `AClickInTheGapUnderARowIsNotAClickOnTheRowBelowIt` | the hit test does not know about the note band, so every click on an explanatory sentence lands on whichever row the arithmetic thinks is there |
| `TheFullestSectionStillFitsThePane` | a row is added, or `ControlH` grows, and Content-with-Hebrew stops fitting - which spec §7 says must not become a scroller |
| `AClickOnAnExclusionAnswersWithItsOwnIndexAndNotWithAControlRow` | the hit test stops at the control rows and the only scroller in the product becomes decoration |
| `AClickPastTheLastExclusionIsNotAClickOnAnExclusion` | the list index is not bounded by what is shown; clicking below the last entry removes `SearchExclusions[6]` from a list of six |
| `TheRemoveCrossIsNotTheRowItself` | the whole row answers `ListRemove`, so selecting an exclusion deletes it |
| `TheListStartsBelowTheRowsAndTheNotesAboveIt` | the list ignores the note on the row above and draws over it |
| `TheCloseButtonCannotBeCoveredByAnythingTheSectionDraws` | `Height` or `FooterH` shrinks until the pane's last row reaches the footer and the close pill is drawn over a control. **This replaces a vacuous test**: the first draft asserted Close "answers before anything under it", and nothing is ever under it - the assertion held however the hit test was ordered |
| `NothingOutsideTheWindowHits` | the bounds check is missing and a negative coordinate produces a negative row index |
| `EverySectionHasATitleSomebodyCanRead` | a section is added to the enum without a title and the rail shows a blank row |
| `TheLabelColumnNarrowsAsARowGetsMoreCrowded` | `LabelWidthFor` returns the wide column for every count, which is what gives five transcription presets 56px each and ellipsises all but 44 of it. Strictly monotonic, so narrowing at four and stopping fails too. **This replaces an assertion that could not pass**: the first attempt demanded 96px per pill from a 546px row which, minus a 150px label column and four 8px gaps, has 72.8 to give - and no label column leaving room for a row label can reach 96 |
| `ASwatchStaysWideEnoughForTheColoursItDraws` | the column narrows far enough that a swatch's accent dot at `Left+14` and ink dot at `Left+30` leave the rounded rect they sit in - which is what tuning `LabelWidthFor` for pills alone would do |

### Task 2 - `PartsTests` (6 methods, 8 cases) and `DerivedTests` (1 added)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `NoWrappedLineIsWiderThanTheColumn` | the measurement uses a different size from the one the text is drawn at |
| `WrappingKeepsEveryWordInOrder` | **the greedy loop drops the word that caused the overflow** - the result reads fine and is missing a word |
| `AWordWiderThanTheColumnIsPlacedRatherThanDroppedOrLoopedOn` | "while it does not fit, emit a line" never terminates for an unbreakable token - long paths in the exclusions list are exactly this shape |
| `AnEmptyStringWrapsToNoLinesRatherThanToOneBlankOne` | the buffer is always emitted; the layout then reserves a note band for every row that has no note and the fullest section stops fitting |
| `AColumnWithNoRoomInItProducesOneLineRatherThanOnePerWord` | **the `maxWidth <= 0` guard is missing.** The first draft asserted only that the result was non-empty, and the mutation it named still passed because every word landed on its own line - which is the worst answer, because the layout would reserve a band as tall as the sentence has words |
| `ANotesHeightIsWhatTheLayoutWillReserveForIt` (3) | zero lines returns one line's worth of air, or the leading is counted once too often - every section then drifts by the number of rows without notes |
| `InkIsReadableOnTheTileNowThatTheSettingsWindowPaintsIt` | `Tile` is derived without the `LightLift` correction - ink measures about 3.5 on Paper, Blueprint and Porcelain and stays fine on the three dark palettes, so it would ship |

### Task 3 - `TypefaceTests` (6)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheFaceTheProductDrawsWithIsTheOneItShips` | the resource is missing, misnamed or excluded from the assembly, so `Parts.Face` falls back to the system default and every surface draws in whatever the host offers. **The failure has no symptom except that two people's screenshots do not match**, which is precisely what spec §9a's "the product rather than a mockup" forbids |
| `TheFaceIsResolvedOnceRatherThanPerCall` | `Face` becomes a property that calls `SKTypeface.FromStream` per access - `Assert.Same` fails because each call returns a new object, and the settings window measures several labels on every pointer move |
| `TheFontIsInTheAssemblyUnderTheNameTheResolverAsksFor` | the `LogicalName` in the csproj and the string in `Parts.Resolve` drift apart - two edits, one of which is easy to forget. The size floor also fires if somebody subsets the font, which OFL condition 3 forbids while it is still called Quicksand |
| `AMissingOrUnreadableFontFallsBackInsteadOfStoppingTheApplication` | `Resolve` lets an exception out of a static initialiser, which surfaces as a `TypeInitializationException` from whatever first measured a string - a missing font must be a product that looks wrong, never one that will not start. Two inputs: a null stream and eight bytes of rubbish |
| `TheLicenceTravelsWithTheFont` | the font is embedded and the licence is not, or it reaches the repository and not the publish folder. **OFL condition 2 requires that "each copy contains the above copyright notice and this license"**, and nothing about omitting it stops the build |
| `OnlyOnePlaceInTheTreeResolvesATypeface` | a surface written later says `SKTypeface.Default` because the one beside it used to. Four files did exactly that before this task, one of them with a comment claiming the real face had already arrived and one of them a diagnostic nobody counts as a surface |

### Task 4 - `UninstallTests` (24 methods, 26 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheScheduledTaskIsRemovedEvenWhenEveryByteOfDataIsKept` | **the task removal is put in the purge branch** - the plausible shape, and the one that leaves an elevated logon task pointing at a deleted binary for everybody who runs an ordinary uninstall |
| ~~`TheTaskIsRemovedWhateverTheQuerySaidIncludingThatItCouldNotTell`~~ | **Cut during execution, along with the `ShouldRemoveTask` predicate it tested, and this row is kept as the reason.** The row above originally claimed the test had "real teeth" because it fails under the mutation it names - and then admitted in its own next sentence that nothing asserts `Run` consults the predicate. Both are true, and together they mean the mutation is unobservable: `Run` calls `HelperTask.Unregister()` directly, so a predicate no caller reads can return anything at all without changing the product. A constant function asserted to return its constant is the can't-fail shape this section exists to catch, and it slipped through into the plan itself. What actually carries the property is `TheScheduledTaskIsRemovedEvenWhenEveryByteOfDataIsKept`, which asserts `RemoveScheduledTask` is in `Plan`'s steps on the ordinary non-purge path, plus the unconditional call in `Run` whose comment now carries the Unknown reasoning. End-to-end checklist step 33 is what proves the task is really gone |
| `EverythingIsStoppedBeforeAnythingIsRemoved` | the steps are emitted in enum order with `DeleteData` early, or a stop step is missing; a helper holding a volume handle is live while files are deleted |
| `KeepingTheModelsAndTheIndexIsTheDefault` | the default deletes - which spec §2a calls "the single most annoying thing this product could do to someone" |
| `PurgeDeletesEveryFolderThePrivacyPageSaysItDoes` | only the three named folders are deleted and the logs survive a purge that `PRIVACY.md` says removes everything |
| `TheSizeInThePromptIsTheOneThatWasMeasured` | **the sentence is built from `Capabilities.TotalBytes`** rather than from the disk. **This test could not pass in the first draft**: its fixture was 3,146,000,000 bytes, which `Sizes.Human` renders "2.93 GB" - exactly what the declared total renders - so it asserted that one string was both present and absent. The fixture is a round gigabyte now |
| `AnEmptyFolderIsStillListedRatherThanFilteredOut` | zero rows are filtered and "why did it not free anything" is unanswerable on a machine that never downloaded a model |
| `ThePromptNamesTheFolderTheRunningProgramCannotDelete` | the report is silent about it and a source builder is left with a folder and no statement about it |
| `ThePurgePromptSaysThatHandWrittenPalettesGoWithIt` | `palettes.json` is swept into the word "settings"; it is somebody's own work and `--purge` deletes it |
| `TheUninstallerNeverStopsItself` | **"stop every process called findra"** - it kills the uninstaller after the helper is stopped and before the task is removed, which is precisely the state the specification calls a defect |
| `TheUninstallerNeverStopsTheParentThatElevatedIt` | `spare` carries only this process. On the elevation path the parent is a live `findra` waiting on the child's exit code, so it lands in `Others` and is killed mid-wait: the exit code never propagates and the command returns silently, half done |
| `TheInterfaceGoesFirstAndTheHelperLast` | the order is reversed, and the scheduled task - still present at that moment - may restart the helper before the removal step runs |
| `AStrayIndexerLeftByACrashIsStoppedToo` | only the two known ids are handled; a stray indexer keeps the index open and the purge reports a failure nobody can act on |
| `AnUnelevatedRunAsksForElevationRatherThanTryingAndFailingQuietly` | it tries anyway, logs "access denied" and exits 0 - leaving the orphan and reporting success |
| `AnAlreadyRelaunchedRunThatIsStillNotElevatedStopsRatherThanLooping` | the relaunch flag is not carried, producing an endless chain of UAC prompts |
| `AnElevatedRunJustGetsOnWithIt` | `Decide` inverts; pairs with the two above so none can be satisfied by a constant |
| `TheFourFoldersAreTheOnesPathsAlreadyNames` | `Measure` re-derives `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Findra` by hand - two derivations of the same four paths is one of them drifting later |
| `AFolderThatIsNotThereMeasuresZeroRatherThanThrowing` | `EnumerateFiles` is called without an existence check and the exception aborts the uninstall before the task is removed |
| `WhatIsMeasuredIsWhatIsOnTheDisk` | `Measure` returns constants, or does not recurse - the prompt then offers to free a fraction of what it frees |
| `APathOutsideFindrasOwnFoldersIsRefusedAndNeverHandedToTheDeleter` | **the containment check is missing.** One `Path.Combine` with an empty segment names `%LOCALAPPDATA%` itself. **This replaces a test that could not fail**: the first draft asserted that paths `Measure` had just built from its own arguments started with those arguments, and could not reach the deleter at all. The deleter here fails the test if it is called |
| `AFolderInsideThemIsDeleted` | `Delete` refuses everything - a perfectly green uninstaller that never frees a byte. The pair is what has teeth |
| `ARootIsNeverItsOwnChild` | containment is a naive prefix test, so a root's parent passes and `%LOCALAPPDATA%` goes with it |
| `OneFolderThatWillNotGoDoesNotStopTheRest` | the loop throws on the first locked file - and the uninstaller has its own log file open in one of the folders it is deleting |
| `TheTaskNameIsQuotedOnTheWayOutJustAsItIsOnTheWayIn` | the name is unquoted; `schtasks /tn Findra` deletes nothing, exits non-zero, and the orphan stays because nobody reads an uninstaller's exit code |

### Task 4 - `AutostartTests` (3) and `InstallSourceTests` (6)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `APathWithASpaceInItIsQuoted` | the bare path is written; Windows runs `C:\Program` at every sign-in, silently, for ever |
| `AnAlreadyQuotedPathIsNotQuotedTwice` | a naive `$"\"{p}\""` makes the entry unrunnable |
| `TheEntryIsUnderTheCurrentUserAndNotTheWholeMachine` | somebody moves it to HKLM "so it works for everyone" - **a constant-pinning guard**, but the mistake is plausible and the uninstaller cannot clean up other users' hives |
| `AMarkerWrittenByTheInstallerIsReadThroughItsLineEndingAndItsCase` | a raw string comparison; a trailing newline or a capital makes every installed copy report itself unknown |
| `NoMarkerAtAllMeansSomebodyBuiltItThemselves` | absence reports `unknown`; a source builder is told to run `winget upgrade` for a package winget has never heard of |
| `AMarkerSayingSomethingElseIsUnknownRatherThanTrusted` | the raw word is trusted; About prints "Installed via carrier-pigeon" and the advice switch falls to its default arm |
| `ADirectoryThatIsNotThereIsNotAnException` | the read is unguarded and a startup stage fails on a path that does not exist |
| `ASourceAlreadyRecordedIsNeverGuessedAgain` | **the shell re-detects at every launch**, which spec §9b forbids; a winget copy whose marker was quarantined becomes a source build |
| `ACopyWithNothingRecordedYetGetsAnAnswerNow` | `Resolve` always returns the config, nothing is ever recorded, and every copy is "unknown". The pair is what has teeth |

### Task 5 - `SettingsModelTests` (44 methods, 53 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `EveryDrawnControlDoesSomethingWhenItIsClicked` | **any row has no arm in `Apply`.** The test this plan exists for: the rejected draft drew five such rows and nothing in it could tell. Delete the `Autostart` arm and it names that row; delete the `Capability` arm and it names that one. **It also could not run in the first repair**: the fixture was `Config.Default`, whose `CapsuleX`/`CapsuleY` are already null, so the reset row produced an equal state and no action and the sweep failed on its own headline row. The fixture carries a dragged position now, and **the skip list stays at `Note` and `Text`** - widening it is the cheap green and disarms the sweep for the five controls it exists for |
| `NoControlIsDispatchedByItsLabel` | a row gains `ControlId.None`, or two rows in a section share an id - which is how a copy-edit to a display string silently unwires a control |
| `EverySectionFitsTheFixedPaneWithItsNotesInIt` | a row or a note grows past the pane. **Counting rows is not enough** - the About disclosure is four lines and a row count sees a six-row section either way |
| `ARowWithNoNoteReservesNoRoomForOne` | `NoteLines` reports one line for an empty note, pushing every row below down 19.5px for nothing |
| `TheHitTestIsToldAboutExactlyTheRowsThePainterDraws` | **`OptionCounts` is computed independently of `Controls`** and the two drift; every click lands one row out, on one section only |
| `EveryOptionRowStatesWhichOfItsOptionsIsOn` | `OptionOn` is shorter than `Options` - an `IndexOutOfRangeException` inside the painter on one palette on one section |
| `ChoosingADarkPaletteLeavesTheLightOneAlone` | **the model keeps one palette field**; following Windows then cannot work and a light desktop is painted in a dark palette |
| `ChoosingAModeThrowsAwayNeitherPick` | the mode switch clears the pick for the other side and Follow Windows has nothing to switch to |
| `EachSwatchRowOffersOnlyPalettesOfItsOwnSide` | both rows list all six; picking Paper as the dark palette saves and does nothing, for ever, with no error |
| `APaletteSomebodyWroteThemselvesAppearsBesideTheSixThatShip` | `Palette.BuiltIn` is read instead of the loaded set; `palettes.json` works everywhere except where somebody would choose from it |
| `ThePalettesFileRowOpensThePalettesFile` | **one of the five dead controls.** No arm, and the row is a signpost to a path the person has to type |
| `ClickingTheHotkeyRowStartsListeningForACombination` | **the second dead control.** The rejected draft wrote the note "Click, then press the combination you want" and no capture mode, no key handler and no call to `Rebind` anywhere |
| `AChordIsBuiltFromWhatWasActuallyPressedAndReadsBack` (3) | a hand-rolled builder spells "Control+F", which `Hotkey.Parse` rejects - so the chord saves and never registers |
| `AModifierPressedOnItsOwnIsNotAChord` (4) | capture binds the first key down, which is the modifier the person is reaching for; the hotkey becomes "Ctrl+" and the way back is the hotkey |
| `AKeyWithNoModifierAtAllIsNotAChord` | `RegisterHotKey` takes a bare letter and swallows it in every text field in Windows |
| `AChordTheSystemAcceptsIsSavedAndCaptureStops` | `Rebind` never saves - which is what makes the refusal test below meaningful rather than tautological |
| `AChordTheSystemRefusesIsNotSavedAndSaysWhichOneWasTaken` | **the chord is saved before it is registered**; the next launch is on a combination that cannot be pressed and the control that would fix it is behind a card the hotkey no longer opens |
| `LeavingTheSectionStopsListening` | capture is a mode nothing cancels, and every key the person presses afterwards is read as a hotkey |
| `TheAutostartToggleActuallyAsksForTheRegistryValueToChange` | **the third dead control**, and the arm handling both directions with one call is a toggle that turns on and never off |
| `TheHelperCanBeRegisteredFromHereWheneverItIsNotKnownToBeThere` (3) | `Unknown` is treated as fine - the case that leaves a locked-down machine with no route back |
| `PressingRegisterItActuallyAsksForTheTaskToBeRegistered` | **the fourth dead control, and the worst**: the recovery path for the defect this plan exists to fix, dispatched in the rejected draft on the literal string `"Register it"` |
| `NoDriveTickedMeansEveryFixedVolumeAndSaysSo` | empty renders as "none" and the person reads that their machine indexes nothing while it indexes everything |
| `TheAddFolderRowAsksForTheOsDialog` | no arm; the only native control spec §7 asks for is unreachable |
| `AFolderAddedToTheSkipListIsOneTheIndexerActuallySkips` | **the picker's leaf name is stored rather than its path** - `\Games\` then excludes `C:\Work\Games` too, which the second assertion catches |
| `TheSameFolderAddedTwiceIsOneEntry` | a bare append; the list grows every time somebody re-picks the same folder |
| `RemovingTheLastExclusionLeavesAnEmptyListRatherThanNothingAtAll` | `null` is assigned and the indexer reads it on the next file, in another process, minutes later |
| `ScrollingTheExclusionsStopsAtTheLastPageRatherThanRunningOffTheEnd` | the clamp uses `count` instead of `count - rows` and the end of the list is empty |
| `TheThreeThingsAPausedIndexCanMeanAreThreeDifferentSentences` | one sentence, or two; somebody who never turned content indexing on reads "paused" and looks for a resume button |
| `ThereIsExactlyOneSwitchForContentIndexing` | a second "paused" bit appears beside `IndexContent`; the equality assertion catches any other field moving |
| `TheTranscriptionPresetsAreTheOneNumberAndNotASecondSetting` | the preset index is stored instead of the minutes |
| `ATypedLimitIsShownAsItselfRatherThanRoundedToAPreset` | a preset enum kept beside the number loses a typed 17 or shows 30 while transcribing 17 |
| `EveryCapabilityRowShowsWhatItWouldAddToWhatIsAlreadyInstalled` | **a fixed per-capability table** - both assertions read the same number, and the total visibly fails to add up |
| `PressingACapabilityRowAsksForThatCapabilityAndNotAnother` | **the fifth dead control**; after first run the only route to a capability was a command line a person in the settings window will not find |
| `AnInstalledCapabilityIsNotOfferedAgain` | the row stays a button and offers to install what is installed. Pairs with the test above |
| `HebrewIsNotOnTheScreenWhereTheMachineHasNoHebrew` | the row is always built and a 1.5 GB decision is put to somebody who cannot use it |
| `TheAboutLineNamesTheActionThatMatchesHowThisCopyWasInstalled` (3) | one fixed sentence; the source case then tells somebody to run `winget upgrade` for a package winget has never heard of |
| `AnUpToDateBuildIsNotOfferedAnUpgradeCommand` | `AboutUpdateLine` calls `Advice` unconditionally and offers an upgrade to the newest version |
| `TheUpdateToggleCarriesTheDisclosureAndNotJustASwitch` | the note is dropped, and the thing spec §9b says is written down rather than buried is buried |
| `CheckNowAsksForACheckRatherThanRedrawingTheSameSentence` | no arm - a button that does nothing beside a tray item that works |
| `TurningTheCheckOffHereMeansNoRequestIsMadeEvenWhenSomebodyForcesOne` | the toggle writes a different field, or a later "Check now" bypasses the config. The fetch throws if called and `force: true` is the strongest path |
| `EveryOptionLabelFitsThePillItIsDrawnIn` | a label grows, or `LabelWidthFor` widens, until a pill ellipsises its own option. Measured with `Parts.Face` - the face the painter is handed - over Choice rows only, because a Swatch draws colours and its options are palette names a stranger's `palettes.json` can make any length. **The first attempt at this test was red on this machine and a different colour on the next**: it measured with `SKTypeface.Default` against labels that did not fit, "Follow Windows" needing about 92px in an 87px pill and "30 minutes" about 65px in a 61px one |
| `EveryRowLabelFitsItsOwnColumn` | `LabelWidthFor` narrows until a row label ellipsises. **This is the pair, and without it the test above is satisfiable by moving the column all the way left**; with it, narrowing to fit the pills breaks the labels and widening to fit the labels breaks the pills |
| `BringingTheCapsuleBackClearsItsPositionAndAsksForItToBeMovedNow` | the arm returns only a config change. The capsule window is built from the config when the shell starts, so without the action an off-screen capsule stays off-screen until the next launch - and being off-screen is the only reason anybody presses this row |
| `EveryStringInEverySectionReadsTheSameOnEveryMachine` | a bare `{n:N0}` anywhere. **Twice repaired.** The first draft read one section and only `Value`, and the single culture-sensitive format is a count, in a `Note`, in Searches. The second read every section and both fields but kept a fixture of the ~38 default exclusions - and `38` formatted `N0` is `38` under de-DE exactly as under the invariant culture, so dropping the `Fixed` argument still passed. The fixture is 1,200 entries now, because a group separator needs four digits |

Driven off the enum, so it fires on the commit that adds one - and the default arm throws rather than returning quietly, because a silent default is what let five controls hide. `RecentreCapsule`, which the second review's finding added, was covered by it the moment the member existed |

### Task 8 - `FirstRunTests` (28 methods, 32 cases)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `EveryThingYouCanTouchOnThisScreenDoesSomething` | any tile, row or switch has no arm - the same sweep the settings model carries, for the same reason. **Compares by content rather than by record equality**: `FirstRunState.Chosen` is an `IReadOnlySet`, record equality on it is reference equality, and every arm returns a fresh `HashSet` - so `Assert.NotEqual` was satisfied by the allocation whatever was in it. `Differs` compares the set's members and the three switches. "Not now" and "Get these" are deliberately not swept: `Apply` has no arm for either, they are answered by the window, and the checklist is what covers them |
| `ThreePresetsAcrossTheTopAndOneClickDecidesIt` | a preset selects an unclosed set, or the tiles are not three |
| `JustNamesCostsNothingAndEverythingIsTheNumberEverySurfaceQuotes` | the totals are typed rather than read, and the screen, the README and the manifest can disagree |
| `JustNamesTurnsContentIndexingBackOffAfterAnotherPresetTurnedItOn` | **the preset arm latches `ContentOn \|\| index > 0`.** Somebody who tries Recommended and settles on Just names gets every drive walked and every document opened - against spec §6, `PRIVACY.md` and Plan 5's headline change |
| `TouchingAnyRowMovesThePresetToCustom` | the tile stays lit and the screen says "Recommended, 900 MB" while fetching 1.4 GB |
| `TickingSpeechTakesTheDocumentModelsItCannotWorkWithout` | `Apply` stores the raw set; Speech installs and answers nothing |
| `UntickingSpeechUnticksTheHebrewSecondPassThatDependsOnIt` | **a naive `Remove`** leaves 1.5 GB that installs and does nothing |
| `HebrewSitsUnderSpeechAndIsIndentedUnderIt` | rows built in enum order make Hebrew read as an alternative rather than a second pass |
| `HebrewIsNotOnTheScreenAtAllWhereTheMachineHasNoHebrew` | the row is shown disabled and a 1.5 GB decision is put to somebody who cannot use it |
| `TheDocumentsRowSaysFreeAndSaysWhichKindOfFree` | the row says only "free"; it costs no download and still does not run until content indexing is on |
| `EveryRowsSizeIsWhatItAddsToWhatIsAlreadyTicked` | **a fixed per-row table** - both assertions read the same number |
| `ATickedRowCostsNothingMoreToKeep` | the marginal ignores what is ticked and a ticked row still shows its full price |
| `TheTotalCountsAModelSharedByTwoCapabilitiesOnce` | the screen sums per-capability totals; the e5 pair is counted twice and the number is bigger than the download |
| `TickingACapabilityTurnsTheContentSwitchOnWhereThePersonCanSeeIt` | the switch is set silently in `Outcome` (consent nobody saw) or not at all (models that read nothing) |
| `TurningTheContentSwitchOffWithCapabilitiesChosenSaysWhatWillActuallyHappen` | the summary is a constant and does not mention the disagreement |
| `TheUpdateCheckIsDisclosedOnThisScreen` | the disclosure is trimmed to "check for updates", which spec §9b and `PRIVACY.md` both promise it is not |
| `TurningTheCheckOffHereMeansTheRequestIsNeverMade` | the toggle is cosmetic and `Outcome` ignores it, or a forced check bypasses the config |
| `NotNowIsACompleteAnswerAndTheScreenDoesNotComeBack` | `FirstRunDone` is only written on the download path and everybody who said no is asked again at every launch |
| `TheScreenIsAnsweredWhenThePersonAnswersItAndNotWhenTheDownloadFinishes` | `FirstRunDone` waits for the last byte and a reboot mid-download brings the screen back |
| `TheOneElevatedThingIsRegisteredWhateverElseWasChosen` (3) | `Unknown` is treated as registered and the machine keeps a permanently empty name index |
| `ProgressIsCountedPerCapabilityAndNotAsOneBar` | one aggregate bar, and a resume counted twice reads "8.4 GB of 2.9 GB" |
| `AFinishedDownloadNamesNoCapabilityAtAll` | **`FirstOrDefault` over a sequence of structs assigned to a nullable is never null**, so the finished screen reads "2 of 2 done - Photos" for ever, naming whichever capability is enum value zero. Both of the first draft's progress tests had an incomplete entry, so neither could see it |
| `AFileAlreadyOnDiskCountsAsDoneRatherThanAsNothing` | only this run's transferred bytes are counted and a machine with one capability complete reads "0 of 2 done" |
| `ADownloadThatFailedSaysSoOnTheScreen` | `Problem` is written by nothing and shown by nothing - which is what the first draft had, so a dropped network was a bar that stopped |
| `EverySizeOnTheScreenReadsTheSameOnEveryMachine` | a bare format string anywhere in `Rows` or `Summary` |
| `EverythingFitsTheScreenWithHebrewOffered` | a row is added, or `RowH` grows, and the switches are drawn over the summary or the buttons |
| `ThereIsARealDeadZoneBetweenTheLastRowAndTheFirstSwitch` (2) | **`RowsToSwitchesGap` is smaller than a row band**, so the notional next row and the first switch interleave and a click one row past the end lands on the content toggle. **The first draft's version of this test could not pass**: it asserted one contrived point that was inside the first switch, against the correct implementation. Walking the interval is what catches it either way |
| `EachPresetTileAnswersWithItsOwnIndex` | the tiles are laid out with a gap the hit test does not know about, and the middle tile answers for the first |

### Task 8 - `FirstRunDownloadTests` (5)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheGateIsRunOnceAfterEverythingRatherThanOncePerFile` | per file, a 2.9 GB install re-queues the whole disk seven times |
| `ADownloadThatThrowsIsReportedRatherThanThrown` | **the controller calls `GetAllAsync` and adds no `try`.** Plan 5's downloader catches `RangeRefusedException` and `IOException` only, so an `HttpRequestException` escapes it, escapes the screen, and escapes an unobserved background task. **The first draft's two failure tests could not pass for exactly this reason** - the `await` threw before anything could be asserted |
| `OneFileThatFailsDoesNotStopTheNextOne` | the set aborts on the first failure and everything after it goes unfetched over one bad mirror |
| `NothingIsQueuedWhenNothingArrived` | the callback is unconditional; a re-queue after a total failure costs an hour of disk for no new answers |
| `CancellingIsNotAFailureToReport` | cancellation is swallowed into a `Problem`, and quitting Findra mid-download puts "the operation was canceled" on screen as though something went wrong |

### Task 9 - `InstallerScriptTests` (12)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheInstallDirectoryCarriesNoVersionNumber` | `DefaultDirName={autopf}\Findra\{#AppVersion}` - the natural thing to write, and every upgrade then points an elevated logon task at a binary that is gone |
| `TheApplicationIdIsAFixedGuidAndNotSomethingThatMoves` | the version is interpolated into `AppId` and every release installs beside the last, registering two scheduled tasks |
| `TheInstallerAsksForAdministratorRights` | `PrivilegesRequired=lowest`, which produces an uninstaller that cannot remove the task it exists to remove |
| `TheVersionIsPassedInRatherThanBakedIn` | a literal version is written into the script - a second place the version lives |
| `TheProcessesAreStoppedBeforeAnyFileIsReplaced` | the stop moves to `ssPostInstall`, after the files are replaced, or is left to `CloseApplications`, which cannot see two of Findra's three processes. **The first draft's version could not pass**: its regex captured only `PrepareToInstall`'s body, and `--stop` is in `StopFindra`, a different routine |
| `TheUninstallerRunsFindrasOwnUninstallLogicRatherThanJustDeletingFiles` | the `[UninstallRun]` entry is dropped and the uninstaller deletes files and leaves the scheduled task |
| `TheUninstallerOffersARealCheckboxAndNotJustAQuestion` | there is no `TNewCheckBox`, or it starts ticked. **The first draft built a message box and this test grepped for `--purge`**, which cannot tell a checkbox from a comment - and both policy pages promise a checkbox |
| `TheUninstallPromptCarriesTheMeasuredSizeRatherThanAVagueWarning` | the form is built without reading the dry-run report, and the installer estimates the size itself |
| `NoModelIsShippedInsideTheInstaller` | a model is added to `[Files]`. **The first draft's version could not pass**: it searched the whole section, whose opening comment contains the word "models" twice. Only the `Source:` lines are read now |
| `TheInstallerRecordsHowFindraGotOntoTheMachine` | the marker is not written, or the switch is not read, and every winget install reports itself as a manual one |
| `TheInstallerDoesNotWriteTheAutostartEntryItself` | somebody adds the "obvious" `[Registry]` Run entry; an elevated installer's HKCU is the elevating admin's hive. **A guard** - the entry is absent today |
| `NothingInTheInstallerClaimsTheBinariesAreSigned` | **guard.** Fires only if the word is added, which is what happens when boilerplate is copied from a signed project |

### Task 10 - `WorkflowTests`, the CI half (7)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `EveryPushAndEveryPullRequestIsBuiltAndTested` | a `branches:` filter is added and a branch pushed without a pull request is never built. The `DoesNotContain` is what makes this more than a word search |
| `TheBuildIsTheOneThatFailsOnAWarning` | `-warnaserror` is dropped to get a build green |
| `TheHeadlessDiagnosticsRunOnEveryPush` | the step is removed and a mode rots until somebody needs it |
| `TheDiagnosticsCheckCoversEveryModeTheProgramAdvertises` | a mode is added to `Program.Main` and not to the script - extracted from the switch itself, so it fails on the commit that adds the mode |
| `NoWorkflowEverRunsAnUninstallThatIsNotADryRun` | a real `--uninstall` is put in a workflow somebody later copies onto a self-hosted machine |
| `NoWorkflowExpandsAnEnvironmentVariableWindowsSpellsWithBrackets` | `$env:ProgramFiles(x86)` - PowerShell ends the name at the `(`, producing `C:\Program Files(x86)\...`, a path that does not exist. **The first draft filed this under "only knowable on the first tag"; it is knowable by reading** |
| `EveryPowerShellBlockInEveryWorkflowParses` | any syntax error in a script no machine here can run. A parse without execution, which is the cheap half of what a reading would do |

### Task 11 - `WorkflowTests`, the release half (8)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `OnlyAVersionTagStartsARelease` | a `branches:` trigger is added and every push to main publishes whatever version the props file holds |
| `NothingIsPublishedBeforeTheTagHasBeenCheckedAgainstTheVersionAndTheChangelog` | the gate is called but the release job does not depend on it |
| `TheReleaseNotesAreTheChangelogSectionAndNotAListOfCommits` | `generate_release_notes: true` makes `CHANGELOG.md` decorative, and it is where the update check sends source builders |
| `BothArchitecturesAreBuiltFromTheFirstRelease` | arm64 is dropped "for now", which means a second manifest lineage in a repository somebody else owns when it returns |
| `ThePublishIsSelfContainedAndTheRuntimeIdentifierIsOnlyOnACommandLine` | `--self-contained` is dropped and a stranger meets an "install .NET first" prompt, or a RID enters a project file |
| `TheSigningSeamExistsAndIsHonestAboutDoingNothingYet` | the step is deleted, or stops saying it does nothing - which is worse than not having it, and which `PolicyPageTests` now couples to the policy page |
| `NothingInTheReleaseWorkflowTouchesTheCatalogue` | a "while we are here" `wingetcreate` step is added to the release - the one action in this plan that cannot be undone |
| `NothingInTheReleaseClaimsTheArtefactsAreSigned` | **guard** |

**Three of these could not fail as written.** Two were found by making the breaking edit and
watching the test stay green; the third by reading. All three are corrected in the same commit as
the workflow:

- `BothArchitecturesAreBuiltFromTheFirstRelease` searched the whole file for `win-arm64`, and the
  comment above the publish step says the RID on the command line is what keeps win-arm64
  reachable. Deleting the arm64 matrix row left the test green. It reads the `matrix:` block now.
- `ThePublishIsSelfContainedAndTheRuntimeIdentifierIsOnlyOnACommandLine` searched the whole file
  for `Publish.ps1`, which the comment above the step also names. Replacing the call with a bare
  `dotnet publish` left it green. It reads the workflow with its comment lines removed now - and
  the half of its own name about self-containment was asserted nowhere at all, so it also reads
  `--self-contained` out of `build/Publish.ps1`, which is the only place that switch lives.
- `NothingIsPublishedBeforeTheTagHasBeenCheckedAgainstTheVersionAndTheChangelog` ended in a bare
  `Assert.Contains("needs:", Release)`. The build job has a `needs:` of its own, so deleting the
  publish job's left the test green with the release running beside the gate rather than after
  it. It names the job that runs the gate, names the job that creates the release, and reads
  that one's `needs:` now.

Two more things this task found, both in files it did not own:

- **Task 10's `EveryPowerShellBlockInEveryWorkflowParses` extracted its blocks with `(?ms)`**, so
  the dot in the body crossed newlines and the first `run: |` block swallowed the rest of the
  file. `ci.yml` has no block scalar at all, so nothing had ever exercised the repetition. Its
  own count guard caught this the moment `release.yml` arrived with two: "2 run: | block(s)
  written, 1 read". The `s` flag is gone.
- **The YAML this task's step 3 prescribes does not parse as PowerShell**, and would have failed
  two of Task 10's tests on arrival. `${{ ... }}` pasted into a `run: |` block is a PowerShell
  syntax error - the parser asks for an escaped brace - and the comment explaining the bracket
  rule spells the wrong form out literally, which is exactly what
  `NoWorkflowExpandsAnEnvironmentVariableWindowsSpellsWithBrackets` forbids. Every GitHub
  expression the file needs inside a shell block arrives through a step-level `env:` instead,
  which is also the form that keeps a tag name from ending the statement it was an argument to.

### Task 12 - `WorkflowTests`, the catalogue half (2) and `WingetManifestTests` (12)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `TheCatalogueIsOnlyEverPublishedByAPersonStartingItByHand` | `release: types: [published]` is added - the most tempting edit in the pipeline, and the one that puts a mis-tagged build on other people's machines |
| `ThisIsTheOnlyWorkflowThatMentionsTheCatalogueAtAll` | the same step is added anywhere else; catches what the test above cannot, because that one reads one file |
| `TheManifestsThisRepositoryKeepsAreTheOnesThatGetPublished` | the workflow uses `wingetcreate update`, which cannot make a first submission and leaves the description and the install switch in somebody else's repository afterwards - **with the other eleven tests still green on a copy nobody publishes** |
| `NoPlaceholderHashCanReachTheCatalogue` | the repository copy carries plausible-looking hex instead of zeros, or the workflow drops the check that no placeholder survived |
| `TheThreeManifestsAgreeAboutWhichVersionTheyDescribe` | one file is bumped and another is not; winget rejects the set after the pull request is open, in somebody else's repository |
| `ThePackageIdentifierIsTheOneFindraTellsPeopleToType` | either side is renamed and `winget upgrade blakazulu.Findra` fails for everybody following Findra's own advice |
| `OneVersionCarriesBothArchitectures` | one installer entry, or two `PackageVersion` keys; somebody on ARM is offered an x64 build or nothing |
| `TheInstallerTypeIsTheOneThatIsActuallyBuilt` | the type is declared `exe` and winget passes the wrong silent switches, so an unattended install opens a wizard and hangs |
| `TheManifestTellsFindraItCameFromWinget` | the custom switch is dropped and every winget install is told to download a file by hand |
| `TheListingCarriesTheModelSizeAsTextBecauseThereIsNoInstallTimeUi` | the size is typed by hand and drifts to the floor total, or is dropped - and there is no install-time UI on either route |
| `TheListingSaysTheOptionalDownloadsAreOptional` | the sentence around the number is lost and 2.93 GB reads as the package's own size |
| `TheListingNamesNoCompetitor` | **guard**, sharing one list with the README test |
| `TheListingDoesNotClaimTheInstallersAreSigned` | **guard** |
| `TheLicenceAndTheAttributionAreDeclared` | either is dropped; Apache-2.0 was chosen over MIT for the propagating attribution (spec §11) |

### Task 13 - `ReadmeTests` (12)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `NoNamedCompetitorAppearsInTheReadme` | **guard.** It fires only when a listed name is written, and it deliberately cannot list the one competitor whose name is an ordinary English word and one of Findra's own presets - that one is a review item in Task 15 |
| `NoComparativeClaimIsMadeAgainstAnything` | a comparative phrase appears at all - which is what "faster than the built-in search" would slip past the test above |
| `EveryImageTheReadmeShowsIsInTheRepository` | an image is renamed or moved, or the README ships with fewer than the four surfaces spec §9a names |
| `EveryImageRecordsTheCommandThatProducedIt` | an image is pasted without its command, and becomes a mockup with an alibi |
| `EveryCommandTheReadmeTellsYouToRunIsOneFindraActuallyHas` | a mode is renamed - the known set is read out of `Program.cs`, so it fails on the commit that renames it |
| `TheReadmeCarriesAMeasuredBenchmarkAndTheMachineItWasMeasuredOn` | the fragment is summarised rather than pasted, or the machine block is trimmed as noise |
| `TheThroughputFigureCameFromARunLargeEnoughToReproduce` | **`findra --searchbench out.md` output is pasted verbatim** - the default corpus is 2,500 and this refuses anything under 10,000 |
| `TheModelTotalIsTheMeasuredOneAndNotTheConservativeFloor` | the number is typed by hand as 2.9 GB, which is the floor total |
| `TheAttributionTheNoticeRequiresIsAboveTheFold` | **the state before this task** - the placeholder puts the licence line at the very bottom, which is what MIT would have given anyway |
| `TheOneRequestThatLeavesTheMachineIsStillWrittenDownHere` | a rewrite drops it, leaving the loud promise and removing its exception |
| `NothingInTheReadmeClaimsTheDownloadsAreSigned` | **guard** |
| `TheReadmeDoesNotStillSayFindraIsNotReadyToInstall` | **the state before this task**, and wrong the moment there is a winget listing |

### Task 14 - `PolicyPageTests` (5)

| Test | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `ThePrivacyPageDoesNotSayHandDeletionRemovesEverything` | **the state before this task.** The scheduled task and the `Run` value are outside both folders, so hand-deletion leaves the orphan the whole plan exists to prevent |
| `BothPagesPromiseTheCheckboxTheInstallerActuallyBuilds` | the installer loses its checkbox while the page still promises one, or the page loses the sentence by copy-edit. A coupling, not a grep - and it fails loudly in both directions |
| `TheSigningPageSaysItIsNotInForceForAsLongAsTheSigningStepDoesNothing` | the status note is removed while the pipeline step is still a placeholder - or kept after signing is arranged. **The one surface the four "nothing says signed" tests did not cover, and the one that makes the claim in the present tense.** The sentinel reads the signing step's own body rather than the whole workflow: "not yet" appears in the step's name and in the comment above it, so a whole-file search would keep the coupling stuck in the placeholder branch for anybody who arranges signing and leaves a nearby comment behind |
| `TheSigningPageDoesNotClaimTheIndexAndModelsAreRemoved` | the self-contradicting sentence returns: "Both are removed by the uninstaller" with the next sentence saying they are kept |
| `NeitherPageDescribesAModeFindraDoesNotHave` | a mode is renamed and both public pages give advice nobody can act on |

---

## Execution notes

Task boundaries are drawn where a reviewer could reject one and approve its neighbour. **The graph below is the corrected one**: the first draft drew Tasks 2 and 7 as independent lanes and invited them to run concurrently, while the settings window read `Autostart.IsSet()` and the first-run screen called `Autostart.Set` - both created in the uninstall task, which neither could compile without.

```
Plan 5 ─────────────────────────────────────────┐  (Capabilities, Sizes, ModelDownloader,
                                                │   TranscribeLimit, Config.IndexContent)
1 (version + release gate)                      │
 │                                              │
 ├── 2 (rail + parts) ── 3 (the typeface) ──┐   │
 │                                          │   │
 └── 4 (uninstall, autostart, install source) ─┴───┘
                                            │
                                            └── 5 (settings model) ── 6 (painter + shots)
                                                                            │
                                                     7 (window + wiring) ────┘
                                                          │
                                                          └── 8 (first run)
                                                                │
              9 (installer) ── 10 (CI) ── 11 (release) ── 12 (catalogue) ── 13 (README) ── 14 (policy) ── 15 (close-out)
```

- **Task 1 is first and alone.** It touches every project file and the one property the product reports about itself. Nothing else starts until `dotnet build -warnaserror` is clean on the whole tree.
- **`{2 -> 3}` and `{4}` are the only genuine parallel lanes, and only after Task 1.** Task 2 owns `src/Findra/Settings/{Rail,Parts}.cs` and Task 3 fills in the one property it deliberately leaves blank; Task 4 owns `src/Findra/Startup/`. No overlap. **None of the three needs Plan 5**, so all of them can start against the tree as it stands today.
- **Task 3 must precede Task 5, not merely reach the tree before the README.** Task 5's two label-fitting tests measure real strings against real pills through `Parts.Face`. Run them before the font ships and their margins describe the host's default face, which is not what anybody will see - and tuning a column against the wrong metrics is worse than not tuning it. The widths quoted in `LabelWidthFor`'s doc comment are estimates until Task 3 step 8 replaces them with measurements.
- **Task 5 needs Plan 5 and Task 2 and Task 4.** It consumes `Capabilities`, `Sizes`, `TranscribeLimit` and `Config.IndexContent` from the first, `RailLayout` and `Parts` from the second, and `HelperTaskState` from the third. It owns `Settings/SettingsModel.cs` alone and is the most consequential pure file in the plan - every visible setting comes out of it - so give it its own review even though it touches no window.
- **Tasks 6 and 7 are the split of what was one task.** The painter and the shot states can be tested and looked at; the window cannot be tested at all. Rejecting one while approving the other is exactly the reviewer's job here, and it was not possible when they were one task with five dead controls inside it.
- **Everything from Task 5 onward is a chain.** The plan claims no more parallelism than that.
- **Tasks 6, 7 and 8 all edit `src/Findra/Diagnostics/SearchShot.cs`**, and its `States` list is one line: Task 6 adds five names, Task 8 adds two. **Serial.**
- **Tasks 4, 7 and 8 all edit `src/Findra/App/App.axaml.cs`** - Task 4 adds four lines to the `settings` stage, Task 7 the tray item and the host implementation, Task 8 the first-run stage. Different methods, but run them in their numbered order.
- **Task 8 also needs `Config.FirstRunDone`**, the only new config field in the plan; `ConfigTests.EveryPropertyIsPartOfEquality` fails immediately if the hand-written `Equals` and `GetHashCode` are not updated in the same edit.
- **Task 9 needs 4** (the installer's `[UninstallRun]` and `PrepareToInstall` invoke modes Task 4 creates), **Task 10 needs 9**, **Task 11 needs 1, 9 and 10**, **Task 12 needs 11**. `WorkflowTests` grows across 9, 10 and 11; each adds its own methods to one file, so **do not run them concurrently**.
- **Task 13 photographs surfaces that do not exist before Task 8** and quotes commands that are not checked before Task 10.
- **Tasks 14 and 15 are last and neither is optional.** The policy pages are a task rather than a tidy-up step because their failure mode is a false public statement; the comment pass cannot be delegated to a grep.

**Ownership of the files more than one task touches:**

| File | Touched by | How to avoid a conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `src/Findra/App/App.axaml.cs` | 4 (install source), 7 (tray, palette, host), 8 (first run) | different methods; run in numbered order |
| `src/Findra/Diagnostics/SearchShot.cs` | 3 (the face, and the comment claiming it already arrived), 6 (five states), 8 (two states) | three separate lines. Serial |
| `src/Findra/Settings/Parts.cs` | 2 (creates, and leaves `Face` a placeholder), 3 (fills `Face` in) | one property, written blank and then filled. No other task touches the file |
| `src/Findra/Diagnostics/SelfTest.cs` | 2 (the settings surfaces join the legibility check, around line 68), 3 (the composed card draws in the shipped face, line 197) | two lines a hundred apart, in different methods. Serial, and Task 3 must not be skipped here: its own drift test scans every file under `src/Findra` |
| `src/Findra/Settings/Rail.cs` | 2 only | `LabelWidthFor` lives with the rest of the geometry rather than with the painter, because the column width and the labels that go in it are one decision |
| `src/Findra/Findra.csproj` | 1 (the three version properties come out), 3 (the font and its licence go in) | different item groups; Task 1 is finished long before Task 3 starts |
| `tests/Findra.Tests/Card/ShotTests.cs` | 6 (a test, and the render count), 8 (the render count again) | serial; the count is in a comment, not an assertion |
| `tests/Findra.Tests/Build/WorkflowTests.cs` | 10, 11, 12 | one class, three groups of methods. Serial |
| `src/Findra/Program.cs` | 4 (`--uninstall`, `--stop`) | one task only |
| `Directory.Build.props` | 1 (creates), read by 9 and 11 | no write contention; do not "tidy" it |

**Six things cannot be verified on this machine, and none may be faked:**

1. **Inno Setup is not installed here.** `iscc` is not on PATH and there is no `Inno Setup 6` directory under either Program Files. The script is asserted as text in Task 9 and compiled for the first time by the release workflow. **Do not install Inno Setup to close this gap** - the point of compiling in CI is that CI is where it will be compiled for every release.
2. **GitHub Actions cannot run here.** All three workflows are asserted as text, and two of those assertions - the `$env:` bracket rule and the PowerShell parse check - exist because the first draft filed a deterministic parse bug under "only a real machine can prove it". Their first real execution is the first push and the first tag.
3. **A real uninstall needs an elevated terminal and a registered scheduled task**, and it is destructive. `--dry-run` is what runs here.
4. **A real winget submission needs a pull request against a repository somebody else owns.** The workflow's `submit` input defaults to off so the first run validates and uploads the manifests for reading. **And `winget` itself may not be on the runner** - the App Installer CLI is not reliably present on GitHub-hosted Windows images - so the validate step checks for it and falls back to `wingetcreate`'s own validation with a line saying which ran. A first execution that fails at a step which is not about the manifests teaches nobody anything.
5. **Whether the shipped face looks right to a person.** After Task 3 every surface draws in Quicksand and every label-fitting assertion measures through that same face, so the numbers stop being machine-dependent - but "does this read as the product" is a judgement, and it will be the first time any Findra surface has been drawn in the face the specification asks for. Task 3 step 9 and checklist step 39 ask for it explicitly. **If a label is ever tight, shorten the label; do not widen the tolerance and do not re-narrow the column** - the two tests are each other's opposites, so moving the column to satisfy one breaks the other.
6. **Two judgements need eyes:** whether the settings window reads as the same object as the card, and whether every control does something when a person - not a test - clicks it. Task 6 step 5 and Task 7 step 9 ask for both explicitly, and Task 7 step 9 asks for six named observations because the rejected draft had five dead controls.

Say which of the six you hit and what you did instead.

**One more thing that is easy to get wrong while executing this plan:** `ShotTests` renders every state in every palette, and this plan takes that from 54 renders to 96. If a new state comes in under the 280-distinct-colour floor, **add colour to the surface**. The constant is what catches a flattened derivation, and lowering it to make a sparse new state pass would disarm the check for the nine states it was measured against.

## What comes next

Nothing. This is the last plan.

What it leaves is a list rather than a plan, and the list is `docs/end-to-end-checklist.md` - forty-three steps now, of which twenty are this plan's. Steps 1 to 4 and 9 to 13 have never executed in any form; steps 24 to 26 are the first execution anywhere of code that registers the scheduled task and starts it; steps 27a and 27b are the walk-throughs that the rejected draft's five dead controls, and the sixth its repair left behind, would have failed; and steps 33 to 35 are the first execution of the code that removes the task. Until that list has been worked through once on a real installed build, Findra is a product that passes its own tests rather than one that has been used.

Three things are deliberately deferred and are written down here so they are decisions rather than omissions:

- **Pre-release versions.** `UpdateCheck.ParseVersion` cannot order `1.2.0-rc.1`, `BuildInfo.Normalise` keeps the suffix so the check reports Unknown rather than guessing, and `Check-Release.ps1` refuses the tag outright. Teaching the comparison semantic-version pre-releases is the prerequisite, and the refusal in the script is the line to delete when it happens.
- **Signing.** The step exists, does nothing, and says so; `docs/code-signing-policy.md` carries a status note saying the same, and `TheSigningPageSaysItIsNotInForceForAsLongAsTheSigningStepDoesNothing` couples the two so neither can quietly get ahead of the other. The application needs a release to point at, which is why it is a checklist step rather than a task here.
- **Any weight of Quicksand but Regular.** One weight ships, because `CardText.Draw` implements bold as `SKFont.Embolden` on the same face and nothing in the tree loads a second one. A real Bold would be a second file to licence, publish and keep in step; the decision belongs with whoever wants it, not with the plan that noticed the font was missing.
- **Keyboard and screen-reader support in the two hand-drawn surfaces.** Spec §12 accepts the cost explicitly and mitigates it by keeping the frequently-touched controls on the capsule's right-click menu and by calling the OS dialog for folder picking. It is "worth revisiting if Findra gets real adoption", and that revisit is a new spec, not a task in this one.

**Two amendments this plan owes the specification**, both argued in Global Constraints and neither made unilaterally in the specification itself:

- **§2's "two supported routes" and "no install-time UI exists on either route"** should become three routes, because winget's manifest has to point at an installer and a zip cannot remove the scheduled task. The install-time UI that sentence guards against is the model download, and that stays in the first-run screen where §2 puts it.
- **§9b's three install sources** should become four: `installer` beside `winget`, `source` and `unknown`, because somebody who downloaded the `.exe` must not be told to run `winget upgrade`.
